At the end of the day, this is another offensive proposal for a law amending and supplementing the law on cross-border enforcement of services. The draft law was submitted to the National Council for consideration. With regard to this proposal, the Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Disabled Persons Committee proposes to the National Assembly that it be decided that the proposal for a law is not suitable for further consideration. ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u2	Thank you for your word. Your Honor! The Committee on Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Disabled Persons, as a parent, considered the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on the cross-border implementation of services submitted by the Council of State to the National Assembly for consideration and adoption on a regular basis by the Council of the Republic of Slovenia. The Committee was provided with the following material: a proposal for a law, an opinion of the Government, an opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service, an opinion of the Chamber of Commerce and Industry of Slovenia and an opinion of the company of the Working Group. No amendments were tabled in the course of business. Representatives of the State Council, representatives of the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities, representative of the Legislative and Legal Service, representatives of the Slovenian Institute of Health Insurance and representatives of the interested public, Chamber of Commerce and Enterprise. The Committee was given an opinion by the Legal and Legislative Service, which examined the draft law in terms of its compliance with the Constitution, the legal system and the technical and regulatory aspects. The Committee was also presented with an opinion by the Government, which does not support the bill. The representative of the proposer briefly presented a law intended to shorten the time of submission of the application until the issue of the A1 certificate for companies and entrepreneurs carrying out their work in the territory of other European Union countries. The representative of the Legislative and Legal Service presented the opinion and concerns of the service regarding the proposed law and the Government's representative the reasons why the Government does not support the bill. Among other things, the Government does not agree with the partial regulation of the issue in question and considers that the proposed amendment should be discussed in the context of an integrated reform of the law already announced by the Ministry. In the discussion, the participants stressed the need for control mechanisms for the protection of workers who are assigned to work abroad. The replacement of the obligation to submit a copy of the contract of employment with a worker who is posted abroad only by a statement made by the employer under criminal and material liability would constitute a significant reduction in the protection of the worker and the possibility of abuse. The fact is that reviewing employment contracts is the basis for protecting the rights of posted workers and is not merely a bureaucratic process. The debaters advocated shortening the procedures for the issue of A1 certificates, but not at the expense of reducing control over workers' rights. It was also pointed out that different data systems should be linked within the European Union. It was pointed out that the Government had already announced the whole revision of the law in question and had informed the social partners and invited them to submit their amendments and additions. After the discussion, the Committee agreed that the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Cross-border Service Implementation Act was not suitable for further consideration. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u3	Thank you very much, Mr. Ambassador. I now give the floor to the proposal for a law, which is to the representative of the Council of State to Mr Dejan Crnek to present his position on the proposal of his mother's working body.
ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u4	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen, Members of Parliament! Allow me, on behalf of my colleague Igor Antauer and the Council of State, to briefly ornate again why in the State Council we wanted to propose amendments to the Cross-border Service Implementation Act. The Slovenian legal persons established in Slovenia and registered in Slovenia for carrying out activities and wishing to carry out activities in another Member State of the European Union are together, harmonised at European Union level and demonstrate that form A1 fulfils all the conditions in accordance with European Union rules. However, the problem is that we have been legally provided that the Health Insurance Office has the possibility to confirm this form for five working days. And these five working days have sometimes been too long for those who have to do something quickly abroad; in particular, they are service activities such as editing, field repairs, transportation and the like. We believe that the optimisation of the current information system could also take this step in order to gather all the necessary information in one place; however, these contracts, which need to be added to the review of the Health Insurance Institute, are still needed. This is being reviewed by the co-workers of the institution, sometimes in a foreign language that they may not even know; and therefore the procedures for this approval are much longer. We understand that there may be a need for any abuse, but also in the Government's opinion it has been said that these abuses have been around 3,000 so far, this is also a number drawn from two different databases. Some numbers are also doubling, and the fact is that these are about 3,000 less than 10% of all contracts being made. We believe that there are other mechanisms for these offenders to be punished if they work in bad workers. We believe that it is with this rapid release of form A1 that we could make a better environment for many workers who are also waiting to be sent abroad. At the same time, I would like to say that we believe that we should slowly write laws for those who are good; not in the sense that we are writing laws, that we are protecting because there are some offenders, and we are not punishing these offenders, but we are turning the whole system upside down so that there are as few violations as possible. The aim of the bill is to fully inform and automate the issue of the A1 certificate via e-VEM and thereby shorten the time and submission of the full application until the issue of the A1 certificate for entities that regularly fulfil all legal obligations, regardless of whether the application has been submitted during or outside the business time of the health insurance institution; the proposal of the law still follows the objective that the worker remain involved in the compulsory insurance of the Republic of Slovenia at the time of work in the territory of another European Union. At the same time, I would ask that the opinion of the Council of State be taken into account and that this law is supported. Once again, we are looking at this law speeding up procedures so that more companies can carry out their orders abroad faster, and more workers could be assigned to work where they could improve their social status. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u5	Thank you very much. I now give the floor to the Secretary of State, Mr Tiln Bozic, to present his position on the opinion of the mother's working body.
ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u6	Madam President, thank you for your floor. Dear Members and Members! The proposal for a law submitted by the National Council for consideration by the State Council has been carefully examined by the Government since the law in question regulates the content which was the subject of intensive inter-sectoral coordination and social dialogue two years ago. During the latter, a delicate balance was sought between preventing the abuse of the rights of posted workers and ensuring competitive conditions for the business of Slovenian companies abroad. The Government is not in favour of a bill that is before you, as it is also exposed to within the competent committee. Now a few words about concrete solutions. With regard to the suspension of the compulsory application of the service contract: the provisions of the Bond Code do not, as a rule, prescribe the nature of the contract, nor do certain other instruments have the nature of the contract in accordance with the case law, so the Government does not explicitly object to the proposal to replace the obligation to submit a service contract in the procedure for issuing the A1 certificate by stating that such a contract exists. Nevertheless, the Government does not agree with the partial regulation of the issue in question and considers that the proposed amendment should be discussed in the context of a comprehensive reform of the law in question in the coming year. It should also be pointed out that this solution stems from the initiative and wishes of the majority of the then National Assembly to address the many abuses that have been detected in practice. It shall also be noted that the Government opposes the proposal in particular in the work relating to the replacement of the obligation to submit an appropriate contract of employment in the A1 certification procedure by stating that such a contract exists. The protection of the rights of posted workers as a particularly vulnerable category of workers was the primary objective that the drafter now pursued in preparing the law. The relevant employment contract is a fundamental institution to achieve this objective. The importance of continuing to maintain this provision of the law also stems from the fact that the Institute for Health Insurance of Slovenia confirmed A1 during the first nine months of this year that the second largest number of irregularities was detected in the submitted employment contracts. The Government also opposes the proposal to supplement the law by stating that, under the conditions met, A1 certificate is issued without delay and that, in the case of an incomplete application, the party is invited to supplement it, since the content in question is already regulated by the General Administrative Procedure Act, which is applicable mutatis mutandis also in the procedure for the issue of A1 certificates. In view of all the above, the Government proposes not to support the proposal of the law before you. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u8	Nice greetings, colleagues and colleagues. Good morning! We have before us a proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on the Cross-border Implementation of Services, which has been tabled by the Council of State in the legislative process. A simpler acquisition and arrangement of A1 certificates is proposed for employers to obtain for workers who are posted abroad. The certificate is uniform for the entire European Union area and also proves that all conditions are met in accordance with European Union rules in the field of coordination of social security systems. In other words, this certificate ensures that the worker is involved in compulsory social insurance at the time of deployment. The employer shall submit the application for the issue of this certificate via the e-VEM portal and, if all legal conditions are met, shall also receive the certificate within 5 working days of the completeness of the application. Of course, this role must also be given in full. The competent public official at the Institute of Health Insurance shall verify compliance with the conditions. The applicant now wishes to be fully automated in this examination procedure, so that the employer, instead of a contract of employment or a posting act for the posted worker, gives only a statement to guarantee the existence of the documents or conditions required by law. While we understand the objective and purpose of the proposal, we cannot agree with such a proposal. It should be taken into account that such a solution has been introduced by law precisely because of numerous infringements in the field of posted workers. The government has pointed out that more than 3,000 irregularities have been identified in this area during the first nine months of this year, which means that at least 3,000 posted workers have had difficulties. That is why it is imperative that the fulfilment of the conditions is not checked by an automated programme, but by a person who will qualitatively assess possible irregularities and thus prevent violations of workers' rights. It is also apparent from the practice of the three ZZS regional units that the A1 certificate was issued within one to two working days, and at the latest within two to three working days, which means that Slovenia has confirmed this issue very up-to-date. I think we are at the summit of the European Union after the date of issue of these certificates. However, if it lasts longer, this is due to the failure of the employer’s role, which I have already talked about. The employer must make a full application and within two, maximum three days, this certificate shall also be returned. In the Group of Members of the List of Marjan Šarc, we support proposals that bring about de-barcation, but not a significant reduction in the protection of workers and not at the expense of possible abuses of labour law. Therefore, the proposal for a new Law on cross-border service delivery cannot be supported. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u10	Thank you, Madam President. Respected and respected! The proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on the Cross-border Implementation of Services, of which the State Council is the proposer, essentially solves the issue of A1 certificates to workers and workers who perform their work in neighbouring countries of our Slovenia or in other European Union Member States. The proposer of this Act is of the opinion that the five-day time limit for obtaining the A1 certificate is too long and that it exceeds all the time limits that Slovenian companies have in contracts to initiate the execution of the accepted contract or service. It therefore seeks to ensure that companies that comply with all legal conditions should obtain a certificate immediately. Following the checks carried out on the issue of the form, the government found that in practice the form was issued within 24 hours or no later than 48 hours, while at the same time the fact that in Slovenia we have even the shortest deadline to obtain the form A1 between the Member States of the European Union. In the Group of Social Democrats, we support proposals to facilitate the work of our companies, but at the same time we must prevent any abuse that would go against workers or on account of their rights. There is no way to overlook the fact that the Working Group also opposes such solutions to the bill. ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u11	Thank you. On behalf of the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre, the position will be presented by Mojca Žnidarič.
ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u12	Thank you for your word. Good-bye, everybody! In February 2017, the Law on the cross-border provision of services, which began to apply on 1 January 2018, was adopted. The law had to be adopted primarily in order to harmonise the law of the European Union. In addition, in years since the start of the economic crisis in Slovenia, we have witnessed a dramatic increase in the range of cross-border provision of services by Slovenian companies in other Member States of the European Union. Although this was a good indicator of increased economic activity, in the context of the posting by Slovenian companies, there was also a significant increase in the abuse of the referral institute and unfortunately also a violation of the rights of posted workers. Such an economic climate has therefore also required Slovenia to lay down more detailed rules for the implementation of the European Regulation on the Coordination of Social Security Systems, in particular in the work relating to the issue of A1 certificates for posted and self-employed persons. In addition, Slovenia had to implement measures to prevent abuses and violations of the rights of posted workers. As it has proved in practice that part of the posting of workers from Slovenia to other European Union countries is due to the abuse of the Slovenian and European legislation governing this area, in particular in the case of so-called straw companies, we cannot agree to the more relaxed conditions for obtaining the A1 certificate in the Modern Centre. The A1 certificate shows that, in accordance with European rules, the conditions are met for a worker to remain involved in compulsory social security in Slovenia at the time of his assignment to another Member State. The National Council, by amendment to the Law on Cross-border Service Implementation, proposes that the employer submit only a declaration of the contract on the contract concluded when applying for A1 in lieu of a copy of the service contract and a copy of the contract of employment. It also proposes to issue the A1 certificate immediately and not only within five working days. The proposed amendment is justified by the need to remove administrative obstacles encountered by employers in the process of obtaining A1 certificate. Notwithstanding the fact that the shortening of the certification procedure itself would most likely have facilitated the work of many employers, in the Modern Centre Party we believe that this should not happen on any condition at the expense of respect for the rights of workers. To illustrate, 93 000 A1 certificates were issued during the first nine months of this year, with the highest number of irregularities found in service contracts; the second largest number of irregularities found relates to the contracts submitted. By confirming the proposed changes, they would actually allow the number of these violations to increase further. With regard to the five-day period for the issue of a certificate, I should remind you that this is the deadline, which means that the actual employer has already received a certificate; moreover, when the law is adopted by the Health Insurance Office which is responsible for issuing those certificates, it has been clarified that the verification of the conditions for obtaining the A1 certificate requires a certain period of time. Thus, such a period was not set just over the thumb. One of the four fundamental freedoms of the European Union is the free movement of services, but we cannot agree that the free movement of services is possible and implemented at the expense of respect for the rights of workers who perform their work in other Member States of the European Union. In the Modern Centre Party, we advocate and advocate consistent respect for labour law. We are of the opinion that the solutions proposed would jeopardise respect for workers' rights; and this is a key reason why we do not support the bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u14	Madam President. A nice greeting to all Members and Members of Parliament and a nice greeting to all in front of small screens, as well as in front of the cameras. Today we are considering the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on cross-border service delivery. Because it's pretty simple, I'll be brief. What do the proponents say? To automate the issue of A1 certificates; to inform and speed up procedures. What do we say? To create new conditions of exploitation, to distort labour law, to reduce labour rights. Why? Because we live in a Europe of capital and non-human beings, because competition comes first, then man comes. Promoting and subsidising competition also makes it easier for workers to break up and divide. How? The posted workers represent competition for workers in the countries to which they are posted. At home, self-employed workers represent competition for those who are in regular working relationships; legislation tries to regulate this, to establish a level playing field and capital is looking for circuits. And what does that look like in practice? Most posted workers come from countries that have entered the European Union since 2004. The largest number of recipient countries is the old members of the European Union. When we talk about countries that travel workers abroad, Slovenia is among the recorders. We are in third place, immediately for Poland and Germany. In the case of posted workers, construction workers dominate. The European Commission's report on posted workers says that five out of ten workers working in construction in Slovenia are posted workers. For the show, in March 2018, we employed 56,000 people in construction, more than 3 000 of them on minimum wage. Why is all this important? Because some of us want to convince us that today we are discussing technology, information technology, progress; that is not true. We are discussing a class fight within the European Union. What are the general findings regarding posted workers? Those published by the official reports. Let's see. Firstly, the salaries paid by posted workers are generally lower than the salaries of workers living in the country of destination. Overpayments are often not paid and the cost of the labour force is reduced and the bargaining power of the working class in the country of destination is reduced. At the same time, better profits also mean sharpening the relationship between posted workers and those who remain at home. Second of all, working hours are usually long. Workers land on a 40-hour worker and are paid that much, but in reality they work 60 hours a week or more without the right to rest and lunch. Third, safety standards at work. As a result, workers are more tired for long hours, which poses a risk to safety and do not usually have training. There are also no translations of health and safety instructions and standards, often without protective equipment and working under poor working conditions. Fourth time, lodging. Workers are often accommodated in cheap dwellings for which they are denied a part of their salary. They are often separated from the local population and even their colleagues. These are not fictitious or individual cases, this is a constant practice at the expense of which surpluses and profits are created. Infringements were detected by the Slovenian Labour Inspectorate and the Institute for Health Insurance of Slovenia. During the first nine months of this year, the second largest number of infringements was found in the first nine months of this year, namely more than 3 000. Today you want to eliminate these contracts according to logic No Brain – no headache. We are therefore talking about non-compliance with Slovenian labour legislation, non-payment for work in accordance with the legislation of the country in which workers are posted and so on. How many actual violations are there, we can only guess. In Levi, of course, we oppose these practices and call on all workers to solidarity, despite the fact that capital puts them back in competition. The distortion of the Labour Relations and Precarisation Act, which is taking place with forced espas within Slovenia, is now being accelerated. We have the law, we have the definition of a working relationship, we have the rights that come out of it. The capitalist is not interested in this; it is time for workers to become aware of this, as this is the first step toward moving together and turning things around for the better. Europe today is a Europe of capital. Europe must be Europe for tomorrow, where workers will have equal rights and comparable wages. For Europe people – not capital! Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u16	Dear President, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! The proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on the cross-border provision of services proposed by the Council of State in its three articles contains a measure that Slovenian entrepreneurs urgently need. A Slovenian company wishing to provide services in another Member State of the European Union must obtain an A1 certificate for each worker it intends to send, showing that a worker abroad has also been included in the compulsory insurance of the Republic of Slovenia at the time of work in the territory of another country. Currently, a copy of the employment contract is required as a mandatory annex to the application for A1 form. The proposal brings its replacement by stating that such a contract already exists. The declaration shall be made under criminal and material or civil liability. The fulfilment of the conditions for the issue of the A1 certificate may, as a consequence, be verified through the information system, thereby speeding up the issue of the certificate. Christian Democrats trust in man, his abilities and judgment. We therefore advocate a limited intervention of regulation and public administration activities in the autonomous sphere of individuals. We are committed to eliminating regulatory constraints everywhere where individuals can voluntarily agree on each other and to eliminating the exercise of ineffective controls, which result only in an increase in operating costs. Large-scale regulation is often only the result of influential interests that can ensure a privileged economic situation through it, which destroys competition, creativity, entrepreneurship and development. Since the law removes bureaucratic obstacles and allows the process of issuing form A1 to be automated, we will support it in Novi Slovenija.

ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u22	Thank you, Vice President. Ladies and gentlemen, honourable Members! The Government of the Republic of Slovenia has submitted to the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia a draft law on intervention measures in the handling of municipal packaging and waste grave candles. I think that I can speak on behalf of all government colleagues and colleagues when I say that it is by no means our desire to put the laws of an intervention nature into the National Assembly after an emergency procedure; but I can honestly tell you that we had no other choice. The system didn't fail today or yesterday, but years ago. In Slovenia, uncontrolled piles of waste packaging and waste grave candles have accumulated. Public service operators can no longer provide fire safety and maintain minimum hygiene standards. As environmental minister, I do not even hope to say out loud what damage is done daily in the environment and our green Slovenia, when the rain is pouring these wastes into our country and our pure drinking water. For all of this, we relied on article 11 of the Environmental Protection Act. We are forced as a country to intervene and here, on behalf of the environment and our unspoiled Slovenia, I ask you for your voice of support. The law was unanimously supported by the committee in this house, and I really hope that Members will also today recognize the seriousness of the situation and support the law so that the state can intervene in order to ensure that this accumulated waste packaging is part of the reality of each and every one of us. As Minister, however, I will continue to work as a priority to take responsibility for such irresponsible work of actors in the system, which have long created these unsustainable conditions in the field, and to put in place a new system that will prevent them from doing so in the future. The proposed law therefore provides that the Republic of Slovenia has the right to recover costs due to the implementation of the intervention measures described by the companies for the management of packaging waste and the holders of joint plans which have not complied with the applicable rules of certain obligations. Two amendments have also been tabled to the bill. The first part of the SDS Group provides that the period of validity shall be reduced to one year, i.e. up to 31 years. 12. 2019. In this part, I understand and support this proposal. This means a clear commitment that the priority in the first half of 2019 is to open a dialogue with all stakeholders in the field of waste management and put the system on a new footing. In the rest of the work, however, it is not possible to agree with the amendment, as it represents a shift from public procurement as foreseen by the Public Procurement Act. New expiry date, i.e. 31. 12. 2019 is also taken into account in the amendment tabled by the coalition parliamentary groups. This means that after that date the law will be extinguished because it is an intervention measure. But at this time, we need to put the system back on track. However, it leaves only those provisions which relate directly to the possibility of recovery of claims or reimbursement of costs. Let me finish. I never imagined being Minister of Environment and Space when I was 37 years old. Even less, I imagined that my first law, which I would present before you and ask for your support, would be an emergency law; but I was placed in front of a wall. Today I propose the adoption of a law that will clean up Slovenian villages and cities of garbage. This isn't the end of the story. We're going to have to re-write a new waste management system together. I imagined three steps: a regulation already adopted, an intervention law, I hope it will be adopted today, and a systemic solution – the Law on Environmental Protection. When all three steps are taken, we will have a regulated waste management system in Slovenia. That's my goal. I hope we can work together to get to him. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u24	Dear President, colleagues and colleagues! The Committee on Infrastructure, Environment and Space is at its 3rd emergency meeting. 12. 2018 as a parent labour body, the draft law on intervention measures in the management of municipal waste packaging and waste grave candles submitted by the Government to the National Assembly for consideration under urgent procedure. On behalf of the applicant, the Minister of Environment and Space, Jure Leben, stressed that the purpose of the proposal for a law was to provide a legal basis for State intervention, since municipal waste packaging and waste disposal candles were not taken over in a prescribed manner and therefore no appropriate handling of these wastes was ensured. He further presented, as is the case today, the main objectives and solutions of the bill, as well as the reasons that led to its preparation and the introduction into the legislative process. In particular, the representative of the Legal and Legislative Service made comments to those articles of the bill, which they considered to exceed the rules of intervention or were not fully in conformity with the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia. It added that the amendments of the proposer, although nomotechnically improving the text of the bill, did not remove the essential comments raised. The representative of the Council of State supported the Ministry's action in a complementary explanation, as this is the only possible solution at a given time. Representatives of the Community of municipalities of Slovenia, the Chamber of Municipal Economy and the SLOCERO Economic Interest Association also expressed support for the proposal. In the debate, MEPs expressed a unanimous view that the situation in the area of waste collection and treatment was alarming and welcomed the Ministry's action. At the same time, it was pointed out that the adoption of the intervention law was only a temporary solution, which should be systematically completed at the optimal time, and therefore the Minister's support was announced to address the issue in a systematic manner. Regarding the warnings of the Legislative and Legal Service on the non-compliance of certain articles with the Constitution, it was expressed by the coalition Members that in this case it was a matter of weighing between a number of types of constitutional rights, giving priority to the right to a healthy living environment. In their view, the amendments tabled by the coalition parliamentary groups in an important part of the notified nomotechnical non-compliance are being abolished. In the SDS Group, they took the view that they would not object to the proposal because they are aware that the situation on the ground requires intervention. In their view, the proposed deadline for the application of the law is 30. 6. 2020 is not ambitious enough and a comprehensive solution should be prepared and adopted earlier. The Levica Group highlighted the view that a suitable solution to the problem of setting up a public undertaking to deal with waste packaging would not be the aim of making a profit. They also highlighted the problem of the level of the price for collection and treatment of waste. The Minister for Environment and Space has provided additional explanations. On the date of application of the law, he explained that it was realistic in his view, as the amendment to the Environmental Protection Act would cover changes in as many as nine different areas. However, if only a segment of waste municipal packaging and headlights were systematically regulated, the deadline for drafting the amendment of the law could be reduced accordingly. He also stressed the importance of a systemic solution to the problem, but the current situation dictates an intervention solution. With regard to the price of taking over and processing of waste, he explained that in the amendment to the Environmental Protection Act, the price share would be better regulated. After discussion, the Committee adopted eight amendments to the coalition groups and voted on and adopted all the articles of the bill together.

In doing so, we must not forget another point that we are also responsible for the amount of waste by ourselves, not only producers, but also public utilities. So already there are quite a few preventive programs and measures, say: Light a candle less, I have my own bag. More municipalities and businesses are already implementing the Zero Waste Strategy or no waste where waste actually becomes a secondary raw material. But today is no matter what, and all of this is an emergency. The aim of the proposal for a law on intervention measures in the handling of municipal packaging and waste grave candles is to provide a legal framework for action by the State in the context of dealing with the consequences of improperly taken and consequently inadequately treated municipal packaging on the environment and human health. On the basis of the inspection carried out by the Environment Inspectorate, it was found that the amount of waste packaging and headlights exceeded the storage capacity of the municipal public service concessionaire for the collection of municipal waste. The packed packaging poses a major health and environmental problem and the government or the Ministry of the Environment have drawn up an intervention law to at least temporarily resolve the issue. The packaging intended for food storage attracts a large number of insects and rodents that threaten the local population by breaking into houses and transferring diseases. Large amounts of waste packaging and grave candles also have a negative impact on the environment, as precipitation into the earth can penetrate the health of harmful substances that contaminate groundwater and also soil. However, it also has a negative impact on the business activity of waste recycling companies as wastes that have long been exposed to weather effects become useless in terms of recycling. Under the law, the State will implement measures in the management of waste packaging in the event that the amount of unprocessed packaging exceeds its storage capacity in local utilities. The measures shall include control of the quantity of packed packaging to be carried out in the form of inspections by inspection services on the basis of which it will be determined whether a municipal undertaking adequately stores or manages waste. If the inspector detects irregularities in the storage or processing of a particular type of waste, he will prohibit the company from storing and ordering the reimbursement of the costs incurred in the implementation of the measures. The law also provides for the implementation of measures where no prescribed treatment is provided for waste packaging and candles. According to the Minister's announcements, which we have also addressed on the Infrastructure Committee, a permanent solution to this issue will be covered in the amendments to the Environmental Protection Act, which must be prepared within a year and a half; or, given the amendments tabled, this deadline will probably be reduced to one year. Therefore, the proposal of the intervention law will be duly confirmed in the Group of Members of the List of Marjan Šarca. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u60	Dear President, honourable Members, distinguished Members and other guests, welcome! The proposal for the Home Supplement Act, which has been tabled by a group of Members and Members with the first-signed Jozet Horvat, proposes the introduction of a household allowance to alleviate poverty among older women living alone who are 65 years of age or older and who receive a pension of up to EUR 500, or if other funds from the state budget or public funds do not exceed that amount. The purpose of this law is also to evaluate the household work of women who are not considered to be work anywhere, as proposed by the proposers. The opinion of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia is that the proposal for a household supplement law is not suitable for further consideration, for the following reasons. In Slovenia we have a system of social protection, which is intended for socially disadvantaged persons and is financed from the State budget, and a system of compulsory social insurance, which generally guarantees rights when an insurance case is made in relation to the contributions paid. The State provides social security for persons, i.e. women over 65 years of age who cannot afford material security themselves because of circumstances which cannot be affected by social security benefits, namely money social assistance and its particular form, this is an extremely financial social assistance and a security allowance. This is an institution dedicated to socially disadvantaged persons and the conditions for obtaining these rights are governed by the Law on Social Security Benefits and the Law on the Enforcement of Rights from Public Funds. A person who is included in a compulsory pension shall be entitled to a pension according to the contributions paid. Women are granted additional benefits under the pension scheme due to child care and increased informal employment involvement, with the possibility of lowering the age of retirement and higher rates compared to male insured persons. Where a person is entitled to a low amount pension and is socially vulnerable, he or she shall be entitled to financial social assistance and a social security allowance in compliance with the conditions laid down by law. The threshold for obtaining monetary social assistance for a single person currently amounts to 392,75 euros and 577,34 euros for a protective allowance. The government further explains that future measures are also envisaged, and measures are also envisaged to improve the social situation of pensioners. The social situation of pensioners will be improved in the future by regular harmonisation, in accordance with the system law; and by extraordinary harmonisations consistent with the financial capabilities of the country. However, for individuals who are yet to retire, the social situation of future changes will be resolved through a rise in percentage rates, i.e. a dose per dose of all types of pensions. Dear Members, the opinion of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia is that the proposal for a household allowance law is not suitable for further consideration and therefore proposes that this proposal be rejected. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u114	Thank you for your word. Your Honor! With regard to the basic views already presented that the proposal for a household additive law is not suitable for further consideration, the Government is essentially further clarifying the following. A single person who is socially at risk has social security in the social protection system. With regard to the allegations of social threats raised in the debate, I would like to stress that social threats or that social threats have been addressed and, in fact, resolved comprehensively by the adoption of the last two novels of the Social Security Benefits Act. The novelties raised the basic amount of the minimum income to 392,75 euros. A single woman over 65 who receives a pension of EUR 300, for example, can receive a cash social assistance amounting to 92.75 euros and a security allowance of EUR 184.59. In addition to a 300-euro pension, the social security system also receives 277,34 euros and its total income is 577,34 euros. At this point, I would like to point out that the amounts of financial social assistance and the social security allowance received under the amended social legislation have ceased to be recovered since 2017, as had been previously exposed. Furthermore, the Government emphasises that the proposal does not guarantee equality before the law or more favourable treatment of women in pension insurance. As the Government has already explained, measures to improve the social situation of pensioners are also envisaged in the future. With regard to the proposal to decide on the application for a household allowance at the first stage of the Institute for Pension and Disability Insurance, the Government explains: The Pension and Disability Insurance Institute is a public institution and is a institution and an operator of compulsory pension and disability insurance. The recognition of the right to a household allowance is not related to prior insurance, but rather to the poor social status of older women and the evaluation of their household work and therefore it is not in any way a right from insurance, but a right which has a social security nature, so the decision of the Institute for Pension and Disability Insurance as a decision-making body at the first stage is absolutely inappropriate. With regard to the financial implications of the law, the Government explains: The adoption of the law would mean the taking over of new commitments of €78.7 million to €99.7 million under the State budget and the funds are not yet secured, and the proposal of the law is also inappropriate and is not suitable for further consideration from a public financial point of view. With regard to the allegations that the law is correcting the wrongs of women who have stayed at home and who have not held back, we explain. In 2000, the Pension and Disability Insurance Act established a state pension for women and men who did not meet the conditions for retirement. In 2012, there was a cleansing of the pension allowance, which excluded non-contributory duties. This content has been transferred to the ZUPJS, i.e. the Law on the Enforcement of Rights from Public Funds, as a form of social transfer, to which are also eligible men and women who are socially at risk. The proposalr's law means returning back to 2000, which limits beneficiaries to women and singles only, thereby creating gender inequality and inequality in relation to the situation. With regard to the reorganisation of social work centres mentioned above, as pointed out by Mrs Iva Dimic, I would like to point out that in the autumn times, social work centres receive a much greater impact each year on rights from public funds. It is the fall time, we all know that then the school year begins, the new school year begins with September, and then the role of extraordinary cash social assistance in the field of chicken is also launched. This is a huge problem and the staff at the centres must also resolve these issues and these additional roles. This means that each year work needs to be organised in a way that makes a quick decision about roles. This year, the Ministry actively involved itself in order to determine the additional payment. In October, an additional payment of the State grant will be made in December, due to the failure of the Furs information system, for which it was not possible to decide on the rights of public funds, an additional payment of the cash social assistance, in addition to the regular payment on 20 December, will be made on 21 December. The Ministry's responding. As regards delays in the resolution of applications at social work centres, I must also say that the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities is very aware of the current situation in this regard, and we are very careful to monitor the way in which these applications are addressed and the dynamics of dealing with them. In doing so, it should be pointed out that at most social work centres there are no delays in the resolution of applications, this is what happened at the centre in Ljubljana. And it should be noted that it is one of the largest centres in the country, with the highest number of users, i.e. customers. Perhaps I would also like to explain this: the roles that await some social work centres are not the roles that would be unresolved due to the failure of systems or, for example, the failure of professional workers at centres that actually resolve these applications and turn them into decisions. These are the roles that the parties are waiting for, and this is an important fact that we have not actually talked about today. On this occasion, by Mrs Iva Dimic, I explain that the application submitted by the father was resolved within the time limit and that there was no actual delay of the body; we have examined the matter. I could list a few cases when this was not the case — perhaps another time. Since these are proposals that have a significant impact on the state budget, we believe that such measures are smarter to take at the same time as some structural reforms that have a thorough and in-depth analysis at the back. We all agree that we would like to ensure equal opportunities for all to give more money to all, but – there is, however. Research has shown that the general characteristic of demographic phenomena is that changes, in particular in a positive direction, take place very slowly. Therefore, measures that affect the field of fertility must be very thoughtful and, of course, planned in the long term. In doing so, studies have also shown that a greater impact is achieved by investing in the provision of support services for families than only by increasing some monetary benefits, although the latter are also very important. For example, for a third child, I would not have decided just because the company or the state would have given me more than 200 euros at the time of the birth of this third child. Analysis has also shown that in Slovenia the fertility rate has declined in the past despite improvements in family policy, which has only confirmed the results that have for decades shown that in fact the negligible impact of individual family policies, particularly maternity, parental leave and child allowances, has been negligible. That, in fact, this impact on fertility is very small. There has never been a mention worth mentioning of a more lasting positive impact of any family policy action on the rate of birth. Of course, we can ask ourselves if the rate of birth would be even lower if, in fact, there were no such measures at all. However, such measures can undoubtedly help to preserve and improve the quality of life of families. Measures which, however, may have a positive impact on the rate of birth are, in particular, the following. Measures to harmonise work and family life should therefore support family policy coordination of work and family life. Promoting the self-employment of young people, their faster employment after completion of schooling, of course, as much as possible, and adequate housing policies are essential. All of this, however, can be supported by appropriate social and tax arrangements. Slovenia has, by comparison, a very good family policy, supported by a variety of measures. We can be very proud of that. When we listen to our colleagues elsewhere in Europe, we can only say that we can be happy that we were born in Slovenia. However, we do not have a comprehensive view. We will not raise natilityes by raising the nominal one-time child-bearing aid, nor will we raise it if the allowance for a large family is slightly proportionally fixed. An increase in fertility will take place when we work hard to implement long-term measures that will make it easier for young people to make a family. In the Group of Members of the List of Marjan Šarc, we support the purpose of the proposal, but we cannot agree with the content of the proposal as such. Thank you very much.

During the previous term of office, paternity leave and paid parental leave were also gradually extended after a new period of 30 days. A austerity measure was implemented, which was in the form of a censorship in the form of child birth aid. A universal right has been introduced for all families, regardless of the material situation in the form of charity that parents today use for children’s equipment, necessities, children’s food and the like. In recent years, some other family policy stimulus measures have been taken. I would therefore like to point out that we have always supported such measures and we have been particularly sensitive to these issues, but we have resolved individual issues gradually, as the circumstances and the constraints involved have to be taken into account. The bill that we are considering today is addressing family policy issues that are legitimate and will have to be dealt with with with with with all due diligence, but it should be stressed that the legal solutions are envisaged in a way that will burden the state budget of EUR 75 million per year, with no resources being provided anywhere. The policy of increasing the remuneration provided for by this law will not necessarily lead to a key objective for which the bill's proponents are to pursue, in itself, greater fertility. We believe that measures in the area of family policy must be more thoughtful and planned in the long term, with the key role being played by the Ministry of Labour, the family, social affairs and equal opportunities. However, rapid and like changes often cause even some negative effects. Therefore, we will not support the proposal by the Social Democrats. Thank you very much.

Demographic trends in Slovenia are fully consistent with trends in almost all of Europe, so imputing the causes of low fertility to social or family policies or even the absence of the latter is unproductive and can even harm the search for effective solutions. Eligible parents will be able to choose child supplies worth €280 from selected stakeholders, and they will be able to make purchases with more than €280. Aid at the birth of a child is essentially a universal right for parents of all newborns regardless of the material situation of the family, since in this way they spend the resources for the purpose for which they actually belong to their parents. The introduction of this right has been laid down in the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Parental Protection and Family Benefits, which entered into force 17. 3. This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. In view of the delay in the envisaged adoption of the proposed amendment of the Law and the introduction of an automatic IT calculation in the exercise of annual rights from public funds and the envisaged reorganisation of social work centres requiring a comprehensive upgrade of the information system, the good offices provision shall apply on 1 January 2020 when information support is also available for this right. The applicants also argue that the ratio between the lower, the three-child families and the higher, the four- or more-child families is unfair, as a family with three children receives a higher amount of allowance for a large family per child – that is, EUR 132 per child – than a family with five children, where 96 euros per child comes, or a family with seven children, where they receive 68 euros per child. It should be pointed out that the State already rewards, if I may put it this way, any next child, since the amount of the child allowance is higher for the second child than for the first child, for the third and subsequent children than for the second child. An allowance for a large family is an annual allowance intended for a family with at least three children, so that large families, because they have more children, do not deteriorate the material position. If we view the allowance for a large family as an allowance for an individual child, we could say that it is the most unfair for families with one or two children who do not actually receive it. However, families with at least four children also have many other benefits, which I will list below. In particular: a lower annual duty of 50 % of the annual passenger car duty; a family with at least four children up to 18 years of age owned or used by a vehicle classified as second toll class B is entitled, in the case of the purchase of an annual vignette for that vehicle, to one-year-old assistance in the amount of the difference between the cheaper and the more expensive vignette; a tax relief for dependent children is gradually increasing, for example, for the first child of 2 000 436.92 euros; and for the fourth dependent child of 6 thousand 187.85 euros; the right to pay contributions when one of the parents leaves the labour market for protection and care of at least four children. Let me conclude with the position of the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities, which is clearly both at home and abroad, that the Republic of Slovenia has an extremely effective and one of the best family policies in the world. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u266	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Dear Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues! The Wine Act is another law that proves how bureaucratic we behave in this country and how we need to prescribe every matter, every procedure, every step, and many times, we do precisely so that people decide to abandon this or that activity. In the case of the wine law, which was amended in 2013, and whose amendments came into force on 1 January 2014, this is precisely the case. At that time, it was not a question of how to make Slovenian legislation as attractive as possible for small vineyards as possible, how to make Slovenian legislation so that small vineyards would encourage them not to abandon their activities, not to cut down vineyards, how new legislation would make them exactly the opposite, to encourage small vineyards to grow even more what they already have, so that they would not be given additional bureaucratic obstacles around their necks and impose new and new costs on them in the production of wine, but how will we, say, halve those who are playing with the rules and so on. Well, then the change came in order to prevent the pouring of unbleached wine, in particular by amending paragraphs 1 and 2. After this law came this ban. What we are proposing in the Slovenian Democratic Party is to prohibit or abolish the ban on the pouring of uncontaminated wine, and we are also proposing to reduce penalties which, according to the law applicable before the amendment of 2013, or which entered into force in 2014, are too high. It can happen that someone who violates the law is, even with financial means, possibly representing the entire year’s crop. Article 44 states in the second paragraph: "No matter what the provisions of the preceding paragraph, wine which is not originally loaded may only be placed on the market of the final consumer by producers registered in accordance with this Act as producers of grapes and wines, offering wine from their own production of grapes and wines in their production establishment registered in the register, except for premium wines of protected geographical origin. We propose to amend this Article in a way that, irrespective of the provisions of the preceding paragraph, wine which is not originally loaded may be placed on the market by the final consumer producers if it is registered under this law as producers of grapes and wines and if it offers PGO land wine, it shall be recognised as having a geographical indication, of its own production of grapes and wine and of its production plant registered in the register, and for that activity, by registered guest establishments meeting the conditions laid down in respect of premises, equipment and skills if they offer PGO land wine from that area. What we want to do with this law is to encourage the sale of wine also in small vineyards in a way that we do not burden them with additional financial costs, when wine needs to be filtered, when additional wine cleaners need to be added, when wine needs to be bottled and so on. In the following, I will also tell you in some cases that what you're saying is how the black or gray economy got angry in this case that it's not true. We are simply in favour of the fact that the guest houses, as they were before, could also pour wine to those small vine growers who are now in a subordinate position as far as financial expenditure is concerned. So we invite the debate and I believe that in Slovenia, when we talk about a boutique destination with five stars, small wine growing is still important.
ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u267	Thank you very much. For the opening presentation, I give the floor to the representative of the Government of Mag. Tanja Strniša, State Secretary at the Ministry of Agriculture, forestry is not a diet. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u268	Mr Vice President, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! The Group of Members and Members, with the first-signed Daniel to blame, submitted to the National Assembly for the adoption of the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Wine Act. The proposer of the bill proposes an amendment to Article 44 of the Wine Act, which sets out the form in which wine can be placed on the market of the final consumer, providing that the applicable law provides that wine can only be placed on the market of the final consumer. At the same time, it allows for an exception to the fact that unoriginally loaded wine can be placed on the market by the final consumer, wine growers offering wine from their own production, to the final consumer in their production plant. The proposer proposes an amendment in such a way as to extend the exemption for the placing on the market of non-originally loaded wine to catering establishments offering country wine from this area. At the same time, it proposes to limit the existing exemption for wine growers who could offer only non-original non-original wine to the final consumer in their production plant. The proposer also proposes to amend Articles 56 and 57 of the Law, which lay down fines for serious and other offences, by proposing a reduction of the prescribed fines for certain cases. The Government notes that the Articles of the Wine Law were the subject of amendments to the Law in 2013, when the Government prepared a proposal for a Law amending and supplementing the Wine Law on the initiative of wine producers with the primary objective of limiting unfair competition to registered wine processing. In order to achieve this objective, it was limited to pour unoriginally stuffed wine in guest establishments, to simplify the penal procedure and to increase penalties for offences. In 2013, the proposed amendments were prepared by a working group composed of representatives of winemakers, organisations combining 16 large wine cellars, 78 family vineyard farms and associations of wine growers and winemakers and representatives of wine inspection. The key debate in the National Assembly had already taken place at the time of the adoption of the law, in addition to the fines which had been reduced by the amendment of the Committee to half of the Government's proposal and the Government's proposal followed the proposal of the winemakers, to another area, which focused on the prohibition of the sale of unbleached wine. It was pointed out at that time that wine poured from properly labelled containers suitable for food and closed in such a way that it was not possible to re-close after opening, without any notice, was considered to be an original filling and could continue in the inn following the adoption of the novel. In accordance with the basic law of 2006, the unoriginally loaded wine can only be poured in wine-growing areas, not in central Slovenia or in Gorenjska. That the restriction does not apply to the filling of musts and that the provision does not prejudice the loading of unoriginally loaded wine in the cellars of growers, as we have already said. The Government therefore notes that the proposal for a law on the form in which wine can be placed on the market and in the field of fines is intended to establish a state before the entry into force of the 2013 law and proposes even some fines that are lower than those in the 2006 basic law. This proposal does not follow the basic principle of the wine law – consumer protection and protection of grape and wine growers. At the same time, we would like to point out that the implementation of the law five years ago in the field did not cause any problems. On the contrary. We note that the proportion of wines not suitable for sale due to faults or disease has decreased by 80% and the proportion of non-identical wines in circulation has decreased by 30%. These findings of the inspection authorities are in favour of the law in force. For these reasons, the Government opposes the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Wine Act and considers that it is not appropriate for further consideration. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u269	Thank you very much. We are moving on to a presentation on the views of the groups of Members. The first is by the Group of Members of the Party Alenka Bratušek and the Member of the Party Andrej Šushmelj. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u270	President, thank you for your word! Dear colleagues and colleagues! I am honored to discuss the proposal for a wine law because I come from the best possible wine-growing environment, with the largest wine cellar in Slovenia. The story of wine, remember, is a story of success and the great potential of Slovenia. The quality of Slovenian wines is rising and gaining more recognition at international competitions. We have two, up to three winemakers on a scale of the world's 100 best winemakers. Where'd that come from? Maybe even because of the Wine Act. In 2013, the Wine Act experienced certain changes that are the subject of today's debate. The main objective of these changes was to ensure and transfer more effective control of the state of winery and to limit unfair competition to registered wine production. Measures to achieve this objective have, inter alia, focused on limiting the flow of unoriginally loaded wine in catering establishments, simplifying the penal procedure and increasing the penalties for offences. It is essential to draw attention together to who has prepared the proposals for these amendments. It wasn't some kind of money greedy, a state apparatus that would hang to fill its pockets or state budget. Nor are bureaucrats who would not dream about the situation on the ground and write laws that have no contact with reality. They were prepared by a working group composed of representatives of winemakers, associations of wine cellars, vineyard farms and associations of wine growers and wine growers and representatives of the Wine Inspection. So it's the people on the ground themselves. If anyone else could claim to have led some controversial interest in wine inspectors, it is very difficult to imagine that winemakers would spit in their own bowls and help design solutions that would not be good for them. The SDS Group has already tabled a amendment to the Wine Act in its previous government reference, and it was already found that both the agricultural ministry and the winegrowers oppose the amendments. Thus, at the time of their previous consideration of the novelty, they pointed out that they were afraid that the re-authorisation of unboiled wine would bring exactly what the law had limited to a certain extent — a greater unfair competition that they had fought before and with which they were fighting to a certain extent today. In doing so, we must not forget that the fight against unfair competition, after all, was a guide and a fundamental reason for the amendment of this law. It would be unwise to return to the previous condition, to say the least, unwise and reckless. We must also touch on the reduction of penalties that are too high and disproportionate to the offences referred to in the articles of the law. The motioner says that such high penalties do not have a dissuasive purpose, but merely an excessively frightening one. In fact, it is not entirely clear what the difference is or what the applicant wishes to communicate with such an argument. If a person is overly intimidated by something that does not deter him, the penalties should be increased even further, not reduced. On the other hand, it is often heard among opponents of the law that penalties are lifelessly high, but it is forgotten that the potential offender always has a choice not to commit any offence. We all know that the low penalties have never been excessively dissuasive. And that's what led the composers in 2013. It is also interesting that the applicant focused mainly on lowering the penalty for large and medium-sized producers, although it is intended to fight for a small producer in the first place. Just one case. The maximum penalty for a large producer who produces grapes, musts, wine or other products as a natural person has been reduced from €15 000 to €300, and the average producer has dropped from €11 000 to €700 for the same offence and the highest possible penalty for the same offence has remained unchanged, €6 000 500. Does that even make any sense? Given that the penalties have been formulated in proportion to the size of the producer and the extent and the gravity of the offence, it would be expected that similar proportionality in the proposed preferences will also lead the applicant, but this has not happened. It was at the beginning of this month that I was visited by a winemaker from my places in my office and in a picturesque and juicy language he explained to me what the proposed changes would lead to. He also said that he remembered too well the times when one wine was officially sold, in fact it was another wine, and the vine from which it was produced did not even grow on Slovenian soil, but the wine was brought from Croatia. In the Alenka Bratušek Group, we agree with the Government, and we believe that the proposed amendments would reduce both consumer protection and the protection of farmers. Both groups would have been injured. In addition, the proposed changes would lead to a decline in the quality of Slovenian wines, which are making excellent progress on both domestic and international markets and are enjoying an ever-increasing reputation. I can therefore conclude that we will not support the conclusion that the proposal for a law is suitable for further consideration. Thank you.

Not only did the MPs and the Government oppose him, but the winegrowers and winemakers opposed him. As of 1 January 2014, the new Wine Act, which had the primary objective of reducing the grey market and eliminating unfair competition from registered wine production, entered into force. The challenge was to protect both the consumer and the producer of grapes and wine. The proposed amendments were prepared by a working group composed of representatives of winemakers and representatives of wine inspection. Among other things, the amended law also provided for a ban on the pouring of unboiled wine in guest shops, but the wine is still sold as open wine, but must be in a properly closed packaging and labelled. In doing so, any container suitable for the storage of foodstuffs shall be considered as an appropriate packaging. Depending on the quality of the wine, these can be glass containers with a volume of up to 10 litres, and all the way to stainless steel containers with a volume of up to 60 litres. Labels must, however, include data on quality, origin and production. Producers themselves can still offer unoriginally stuffed wine in their production plant. However, fines have increased considerably for violators. The wine inspection, which supervises compliance with the provisions of the legislation in force, finds that the infringement is minor every year. In 2017, for example, only 15 infringements were found. The introduction of the novel did not cause any problems and traffic in unoriginally loaded wine decreased dramatically in the inn establishments. The proportion of non-marketable wines decreased by 80 %. The share of non-identical wines in transport is 30%. Very encouraging results, then! The proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Wine Act before us today proposes an amendment to Article 44 of the Wine Act, which sets out the form in which wine can be placed on the market for the final consumer. It suggests, inter alia, that unbleached wine be allowed again and that penalties for offences be reduced. Since the basic principles of the existing wine law are primarily the protection of the consumer and the protection of the producer of grapes and wine, the re tabled proposal for the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Wine in the Group of Letters of Marjan Šarc will not, of course, be supported.
ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u279	Thank you very much. Ambassador Jani Prednik will present the position of the Group of Social Democrats. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2018-12-18-SDZ8-Redna-03.ana.u280	Dear Vice-President, Secretary of State with co-workers, Members of Parliament, Members of Parliament! The substance of the proposal for amendments and the addition of the applicable wine law is the extension of the exemption allowing the sale of unoriginally loaded country wine. Such sales are permitted under the applicable law only to wine producers who can sell wine of their own production in such a way in their production establishments. The applicants want this exception to be extended, thus allowing for the reselling of unoriginally stuffed locally produced wine in catering establishments as well. In addition, the applicants propose a reduction in the amount of the fines currently prescribed for serious infringements of the current law. Slovenia is a wine-growing country with a high share of self-sustainment and its own production. The wine-making palette is also wide. The restrictions imposed by the applicable law aim not to prevent fraud, abuse and unfair competition. The essence of the current regime is to ensure that the food is used for appropriate packaging when the non-originally loaded wine is sold in catering establishments. Guests must also use the premises that prevent re-closure after opening. This ensures the quality of wines and consumer safety and convinces tax abuses. The implementation of the proposal would thus, in particular, increase the supply of low quality wine in catering establishments and open up opportunities for abuse, such as mixing different wines in guest warehouses. The second part of the bill is the requirement to reduce the penalties in force, which were also imposed five years ago. We agree with the Social Democrats that in Slovenia we often intervene too quickly on the regulation of high penalties with the so-called deterrent effect, but we must also point out that in this case, we cannot be classified as draconian. The height of the fines is determined in the range from the lowest to the highest. However, it should be borne in mind that even violators do not always fall into the category of small winemaker according to the performances of the average man. When infringements bring great wealth benefits, fines must also follow. Otherwise, it is possible to continue the practice known also in any other area when the offender pays the fine, which is only a small percentage of the profits obtained. This does not deter violations, but encourages them to repeat them. Therefore, we also consider that the reduction of the fines in force is not appropriate unless it turns out that they are disproportionately high on a case-by-case basis in relation to the gravity of the offence. Although we will listen to the arguments of the motioners in the Group of Social Democrats, we do not believe that substantial changes to the law currently in force are necessary. Therefore, when we vote, we will not support the proposal for a decision that the bill is suitable for further consideration. Thank you.
