Allow me to say, however, that just as we proposed these amendments in 2013 only when they were in line with all groups of winegrowers and winemakers, this time we also checked the position of NGOs. We have obtained the opinion of the Agricultural and Agriculture Chamber of Slovenia, Association of Slovenia, Association of Family Winegrowers and Winegrowers of Slovenia and Wine Society of Slovenia, and last but not least, the Association of Associations of Winegrowers and Winegrowers of Slovenia, which brings together 35 associations from all Slovenia and many small winegrowers. In particular, with these small ones and with Vinis, we also call this association Vinis, share the concerns that you, honourable Members and Members, share, and mention many times the concerns about the disappearance of vineyards within those areas where the own structure of vineyards is particularly fragmented. We have been working with them for some time to prepare measures to curb this process. That is why we were particularly interested in their position, and they replied in writing that they advocated order in the wine market, so they do not support your proposal to amend the law. They do not support the introduction of the pre-2014 situation, as regards the pouring of open wine in the inn. They further say that they are convinced that Slovenian winegrowers and winemakers must be legally protected against uncontrolled dumped imports of cheap wine from abroad, which is then sold in our stores in an unreputable package at a price of EUR 1.78 per litre, I quote their statements. With regard to Vinis, it is feared that in the event that the so-called open wine can be refuelled in the establishments, there would be abuse and sale of cheap imported wine, which would result in even greater non-competitiveness and lowering of already too low domestic wine prices. I'd like to tell you what the original filling of wine means. On several occasions, respected applicants said that it was an exception, which had already been applied to country wine, or to top quality wine, before or before 2014 or before this amendment of the law, but not to table wine, or to top quality wine, which could and should always be sold only bottled or originally stuffed. I also used the expression originally charged in the presentation of the government's opinion on purpose, because this does not mean that it is bottled in bottles. Here I would also like to say that in a small vineyard, as was sometimes mentioned here, which has 500 vines and would like the vineyarder, for example, 100,200 litres of wine sold to the inn, the law does not oblige him to sell it in bottles. It can sell this in bottles with a chronic stopper, it can be sold in plastic packaging, which is closed in such a way that it can be seen once opened that it has already been opened. That means the way it can be glued with a sticker. It is true, however, that such packaging must be marked with a label that can be made at home, written on the hand, written on the home printer, and the label must be affixed to this packaging. Contrary to the previous arrangements, when someone could also bring the inn wine in plastic packaging to the inn, the glass packaging, which was not necessarily closed initially, had to have a wine certificate now and before, the right assessment of the wine and the appropriateness of the wine had to be in any case. The wine must have been finished and suitable for sale in any case, whether bottled or unboiled, originally loaded or not. By having to obtain a document on the administrative unit that had to accompany this package, who did not have the original stuffed wine. Not a label, but there had to be a document that was valid for only 6 months because the wine was not originally loaded. And this exception was applied only to guest restaurants in wine-growing areas, as I have said many times. So the exception was narrow, and this narrow exception ended the law because the vineyarders wanted it so badly because this exception allowed too easy abuse. The change in beer habits or wine habits also required this. It has been mentioned several times that the Hotel Chamber was against this introduction at the time. When we had a workshop with them after the marriage and explained to them what the original wine loaded meant, they had no further problems. Because before that, it was already about five years before 2013 that the innkeepers asked the winemakers to do so. Since different wine is used in restaurants and if unoriginally loaded in larger containers, it was exposed to deterioration because it was in contact with air. See, even if you applied this amendment you're proposing, the question is whether the guests would agree to this or would actually require the winemaker to bring in the original packed packaging. Because that's what they've asked for before. And this was found in the amendment of the law, that only the law was something that the innkeepers had met before. So far from presuming that a small vine grower must have a filling line. But it is true that a winegrower, if a bottle of wine himself, must be full of wine — someone else who is registered for the bottle — if he is doing so himself, must be registered for it and must also be registered in the register of wine growers and grapes to record the wine bottles. However, the condition is that he or someone from this agricultural holding who is registered in the wine register must have a clerical course. This 42-hour clerical course, which teaches farmers the basics of wine-growing and basics of wine handling, costs about 130 euros according to the information we have from agri-land institutions. That's an extra cost. While in the second place, it had to be in a clean packaging, only that it must now be a label with wine information and that this stopper must be originally glued, it can also be with a sticker. So the other one isn't an extra cost. That's the fact that he has to have a course, and he is. Even after reporting to administrative units, this fact does not constitute an important obstacle because most wine growers already meet this condition. In short, I wanted to explain what the current regime is like, and especially that such an existing regime is actually supported by wine growers and wine makers, precisely those who are our interlocutors who act in an organized manner, also small, especially explicitly, because they are aware that, in the long and medium term, it is only the right path to greater quality and also to their existence. Thank you very much.
